# Review log — 0108_fix_proposal-0061-re-derived-plan-item-gateway-runtime-comms

## Standing context

**Changelog (compaction pass after spec round 8).** This pass lifted the durable residue of every ledger entry from spec.1 through spec.8 into an empty standing context. The ledger itself was left unedited for the round-boundary archive. Superseded and deleted rather than lifted: the spec.1 review WATCHOUTs that SPEC-1 kept "An implicit session record is created per request" (fixed by spec.1.fix-G1.1); the spec.1.fix-G3.1 decision citing §10.2 in paragraph B (corrected by spec.1.follow-up-fix.1); the multi_turn-only predicate and the FACT "Per-tenant overrides are not in spec/" (corrected by spec.2.fix-G1.1, spec.2.fix-design-G1.1, and spec.2.review-mechanism.1); the WATCHOUT that paragraph C said "the adapter refuses" (fixed by spec.1.fix-G4.1); paragraph D's delivery predicate and the WATCHOUT that summary decision 8 stays true (corrected by spec.4.fix-G1.1); the WATCHOUT that a root or cold pre-delivery dispatch failure had no outcome (fixed by spec.4.fix-G1.1); SPEC-3 rows keyed on the final outcome (fixed by spec.4.fix-G2.1); Edit A's "address no pre-existing session" (fixed by spec.6.fix-G1.1); the missing root-turn capture point (fixed by spec.8.fix-G1.1). Closed and retired: the spec.1.fix-G2.1 OPEN on unlisted BindSingleShot implementers (spec.1.follow-up-fix.1 says CODE-5 step 6 now names them); the spec.1.review-citations.1 UNVERIFIED on whether the warm path flows through the idle stamper (spec.1.review-fresh.1, spec.8.review-fresh.1, and spec.8.review-mechanism.1 found that no message-delivery path stamps and the sentence matches §6.2 and the code). Retired as refuted: the spec.1.review-single-source.1 WATCHOUTs on rows 1 and 2 repeating the 503 mapping and on paragraph C's state list (spec.4.review-single-source.1 records them as refuted in history). Duplicate entries across lenses were merged into one line each. The section is about 120 lines, which is under the 200-line target, and nothing was dropped to reach it. The `## Retired` section was not created because the ledger is archived whole at the round boundary; the retirements are recorded in this changelog.

### Settled

- **SPEC-1 ending.** SPEC-1 ends with the bare pointer "`OpenResponsesAdapter` requests follow **Open Responses conversation sessions.**"; the "implicit session record is created per request for audit and billing" sentence is deleted outright (spec.1.fix-G1.1, spec.1.fix-design-G1.1).
- **SPEC-1 locator.** The SPEC-1 locator still quotes the current spec/15 ending sentence, which is correct because it locates the text being replaced; only SPEC-2's anchor changed, and it now names the SPEC-1 paragraph by bold label (spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **SPEC-1 anchor.** The §15 single-shot paragraph is the one bold paragraph directly before `### 15.1 REST API`; it sits structurally under `#### SessionEvent Kind Registry`; locate it by bold label (spec.1.review-applicability.1, spec.4.review-citations.1).
- **SPEC-1 unchanged span.** The "unchanged" sentences from "The implicit session runs through the same" to "before the response returns." match spec/15 verbatim (spec.1.review-citations.1, spec.2.review-single-source.1, spec.8.review-citations.1).
- **Per-request billing claim.** The deleted closing sentence was the only per-request audit-and-billing claim for the built-in adapters; `type: mcp` keeps its own sentence (spec.1.fix-design-G1.1, spec.2.review-edit-sites.1).
- **§12.8 creation fence.** "the implicit sessions of the built-in adapters" stays true; cold and root turns still insert a row through it, and drain covers held sessions (spec.2.review-applicability.1, spec.4.review-security.1, spec.6.review-security.1).
- **Hold predicate home.** SPEC-2 paragraph A is the single home of the hold predicate: held only when `conversationContinuity` is `"platform"` and the runtime, with the session tenant's capabilities applied (§5.1), declares `multi_turn` and `injection.supported: true`; "the gateway releases every other session" (spec.2.fix-G1.1, spec.2.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Why both capability terms.** §5.1 "Capabilities are customizable per tenant" and the §15 SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE row's per-tenant override read put tenant-resolved capability in spec; overrides validate enums only, so effective multi_turn with injection false is possible (spec.2.fix-G1.1, spec.2.review-mechanism.1).
- **No warm condition 5.** One_shot handling is the release predicate in paragraph A; a released session is `completed`, so warm condition 1 sends every continuation cold (spec.1.fix-G2.1).
- **conversationContinuity is binary.** §7.1 allows only `"platform"` or `"none"`, so "is platform" equals "is not none" (spec.2.fix-G1.1, spec.3 reviews).
- **Binder returns hold.** CODE-5 step 6 binder returns `hold bool` instead of `continuity`; `sessionSingleShotBinder` (cmd/lenny-gateway/credentialsurface.go) computes it from continuity plus `runtimecapoverride.ResolveForTenant(w.runtimes, w.capOverrides, ...)`; resolve error or not-found gives hold=false (spec.1.fix-design-G2.1, spec.2.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Binder bullet is a pointer.** CODE-5 step 6's binder bullet points at paragraph A and keeps only the locator and the fail-closed trap (spec.2.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Binder wiring today.** `sessionSingleShotBinder` holds only `srv`; the runtime and override stores are `w.runtimes` and `w.capOverrides` on the wiring struct (spec.1.fix-G2.1).
- **ResolveForTenant behavior.** Returns the base runtime when no override exists; propagates runtime not-found and non-not-found store errors (spec.1.fix-G2.1).
- **BindSingleShot implementers.** credentialsurface.go, singleshot.go noop, openai_chat.go, open_responses.go, singleshot_test.go, openai_chat_test.go stub, tier2 openai_singleshot_lifecycle_test.go, tier7a singleshot_admission_ordering/scenario.go, credentialsurface_singleshot_test.go, caller_check_wiring_test.go; CODE-5 step 6 names them (spec.1.follow-up-fix.1).
- **Docs restatement of predicate.** non-spec-changes docs edit (b) reads "a runtime that is not `multi_turn` with injection enabled for the tenant accepts no second message" (spec.2.follow-up-fix.1).
- **Summary decision 7.** Leads with "A session that SPEC-2 paragraph A does not hold" (spec.2.fix-G1.1).
- **Paragraph B visibility.** Paragraph B is tenant-scoped ("a session in the caller's tenant"); the §10.2 per-owner gap is recorded in summary decision 15 and the Defects list for staged spec and code alike (spec.1.follow-up-fix.1).
- **Paragraph C inherits B.** Paragraph C rejects "an id that GET would answer with 404", and SPEC-3 row 3 inherits authorization via §29.3 Forwarding rule 3 (spec.1.fix-G3.1, spec.1.review-security.1).
- **No owner check in tree.** No session route enforces §10.2's per-session-owner check; `handleGet` checks read_own_sessions then does a tenant-scoped Get; F-15.1.32 defers it (spec.1.fix-design-G3.1).
- **RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND meaning.** §15.1 already means "does not exist or is not visible to the caller" (spec.1.fix-G3.1).
- **Client-asserted user.** The translator sets session UserID from request body `user` (open_responses.go `UserID: req.User`); listed in summary Defects (spec.1.review-security.1).
- **Paragraph C cold trigger.** A continuation goes cold when its "warm dispatch fails before any input reaches the runtime"; the delivery boundary lives only in C (spec.1.fix-G4.1, spec.4.fix-G1.1).
- **Paragraph D residual rule.** D reads "When the transcript append, the completion write, or a dispatch fails, and the continuation rules above do not answer that failure with a cold turn, the gateway answers a native `500`, ..." (spec.4.fix-G1.1, spec.4.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Summary decision 8.** Second sentence is the pointer "SPEC-2 paragraph D is the one failure rule." (spec.4.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Summary decision 9.** A pointer ("A continuation the warm path cannot serve goes cold (SPEC-2 paragraph C).") plus the reservation sentence (spec.1.fix-G4.1).
- **"refused for concurrency" deleted.** Removed from paragraph C, because a cold continuation passes the §11.1 concurrency gate (spec.1.fix-G4.1).
- **Non-delivery errors.** `executor.ErrNotBound` and `SealedError{Delivered: 0}` are the only Send errors that establish non-delivery; any other Send error is unknown delivery (spec.1.fix-design-G4.1).
- **Tree matches widened D.** handleCreate arms the deferred release with DispositionFailed before dispatch and answers a Send error with 500 (spec.4.fix-G1.1).
- **Capture point.** Paragraph D captures recovery_generation "at the reservation for a warm turn, and at session creation for a turn that creates its session"; summary watch-out points at D and keeps only the never-at-commit trap (spec.8.fix-G1.1, spec.8.fix-design-G1.1).
- **serveCold captures bindGen.** CODE-5 step 6 serves root and cold turns alike and captures `bindGen` at bind (spec.8.fix-G1.1).
- **SPEC-3 row selection.** Rows 2 and 3 are selected by warm conditions 1-4 on the serving replica's read of the session row; row 3's decision-timing sentence was deleted as a restatement of rule 1 (spec.4.fix-G2.1).
- **CODE-5 forwards on the read.** CODE-5 step 2 and the CODE-3 doc comment already define warm-eligible as conditions 1-4 on the row (spec.4.fix-G2.1).
- **Edit A wording.** SPEC-3 Edit A reads "the `POST /v1/chat/completions` row and the `POST /v1/responses` row that requires no forwarding are served on a session the serving replica creates" (spec.6.fix-G1.1).
- **Edit A picks one row.** No other §29.3 off-holder row is an Open Responses row, so "the row that requires no forwarding" picks one (spec.4.fix-design-G2.1).
- **SPEC-4.** SPEC-4 adds a [§15](15_external-api-surface.md#15-external-api-surface) link ahead of the §15.1/§15.2 links in the §28.5.4 CH-HOLDERFORWARD **Messages.** bullet; the quoted target matches exactly once (spec.1.fix-G5.1, spec.2 reviews).
- **SPEC-4 kept separate.** Not folded into SPEC-3, whose heading names spec/29 (spec.1.fix-G5.1).
- **Other proposals.** 0085, 0086, 0106, and 0107 stage nothing in the §15 single-shot paragraph, the §29.3 lead-in or adapter row, or §28.5.4; 0107 edits §28.5.2 only (spec.1.fix-G5.1, spec.8.review-edit-sites.1).
- **Spec edit sites.** Open Responses single-shot and continuation semantics live only at the §15 paragraph and the §29.3 lead-in and adapter row; `previous_response_id` occurs nowhere else; SPEC-1 through SPEC-4 cover every site (multiple edit-sites and single-source reviews).
- **Other mentions stay true.** §12.8, §15.2.1 item 5, §21 BaseAdapter, §4.1, §9.1, §10.6, and §7.1 "single-shot" (derive edge) need no edit (spec.6.review-edit-sites.1).
- **§4.2 field list.** Not exhaustive (already omits `state_entered_at`), so response records need no §4.2 edit (spec.8.review-edit-sites.1).
- **Transcript storage.** Transcript entries are Postgres `session_messages` under StoreTypeSession, so §12.8 erasure covers them; the store has no Redis dependency (spec.6.review-edit-sites.1, spec.4.review-performance.1).
- **Idle stamp sentence verified.** "A warm turn writes no idle-clock stamp of its own" matches §6.2 and code: no message-delivery path stamps; agent output, LLM proxy, MCP tools, and the seal answer do (spec.1.review-fresh.1, spec.8.review-fresh.1). Do not re-derive.
- **Sealed session refusal.** A warm turn reaching a sealed session is refused before input, so it goes cold; the idle-suspension race is closed (spec.1.review-reliability.1, spec.4.review-reliability.1).
- **recovery_generation scope.** Advances on every pod restore including podless idle-suspended resume; never on a coordinator handoff (spec.1.review-mechanism.1, spec.1.review-reliability.1).
- **Coordinator adoption.** A lapsed REG-COORDLEASE on a running session is adopted by another replica, so row 3's 409 is a bounded window; REG-COORDLEASE falls back to Postgres during a Redis outage (spec.1.review-reliability.1, spec.6.review-performance.1).
- **Implicit session has a coordinator.** BindSingleShot runs CreateAndStartService, which acquires REG-COORDLEASE (spec.6.review-mechanism.1).
- **Forward streaming.** CH-HOLDERFORWARD relays text/event-stream with flushing (spec.8.review-mechanism.1).
- **Rule 6 split.** Rule 6 enqueues only a message with no inReplyTo; POST /v1/responses falls under "Every other request ... TARGET_NOT_READY" (spec.1.review-citations.1, spec.4.review-single-source.1).
- **GET is store-only.** GET /v1/responses/{id} is covered by the §29.3 store-only row (spec.1.review-mechanism.1).
- **TARGET_NOT_READY catalog.** The §15.1 row is pre-running only; §29.3 already declares the rule 6/7 condition separate, so no catalog edit is owed (multiple reviews).
- **Phase boundary.** CH-HOLDERFORWARD is Phase 6 and the translator Phase 5; not filed (spec.1.review-fresh.1).
- **Idempotency.** §11.5 keys do not cover adapter routes; dedup is the warm-condition-2 compare-and-set (spec.4.review-reliability.1).
- **Session id format.** Session ids are UUIDv8, so `<sessionId>.<n>` is unambiguous; response ids already equal session ids today, so no new exposure (spec.4.review-mechanism.1, spec.4.review-security.1).
- **PreMessageDelivery.** Applies only to `lenny/send_message`, so a warm turn skipping it is no regression (spec.4.review-security.1).
- **Soft disable.** §11.4 soft disable denies new sessions only; full revoke cancels held sessions (spec.6.review-security.1).
- **Native envelope.** The OpenAI error envelope has type, message, and code only; `error.current_state` is named only in summary decision 11, CODE-5 step 4, and the tier-3 test (spec.1.review-client-surface.1, spec.1.review-docs-alignment.1).
- **OpenAPI.** openapi.json models /v1/responses as 200/400 and GET as 200/404 with no id format (spec.1.review-client-surface.1).
- **No SDK or schema parallel.** No sdks/, schemas/, or charts/ file references /v1/responses or previous_response_id (spec.4.review-client-surface.1).
- **Cold-path read cost.** Rehydrate already walks the parent chain O(depth); warm turns shorten it (spec.4.review-performance.1).
- **Envelope growth.** Response records ride the request-envelope jsonb, O(turns), bounded by maxSessionAge; below the bar (spec.6.review-performance.1, spec.8.review-performance.1).
- **Required writes.** Shipped handler ignores the completion-write error; making it required is summary decision 8's deliberate choice (spec.6.review-performance.1).
- **Capacity owner-settled.** Pod-hold and §11.1 growth per conversation follows the 2026-10-28 owner decision; capacity formulas stay valid (spec.1.review-performance.1, spec.8.review-performance.1).
- **Plan step.** The claimed plan step is the "Proposal 0061, re-derived" bullet under "Queue after P4b" in gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md; every deliverable maps to SPEC-1..4 or summary decisions 1, 3, 9, 14 (plan-conformance reviews spec.1, spec.4, spec.6, spec.8).
- **Tier-11 gate.** off_holder_matrix_stated_outcome_test.go pins no string SPEC-3 touches; no gate parses bold labels (spec.4.review-applicability.1, spec.6.review-applicability.1).
- **No-finding rounds.** Reliability, performance, security, single-source, docs-alignment, edit-sites, and plan-conformance lenses filed nothing in rounds 4, 6, and 8; round 7 diff was the single Edit A clause.

### Traps

- **Do not regrow the SPEC-1 pointer.** Do not expand the pointer back into a description of SPEC-2 (pod hold, warm/cold continuation, which requests create a session). That description made every request "create its session". Withdrawn alternatives: rescoping the sentence to Chat Completions (restates SPEC-1's opening), "a request that creates a session does so under these gates" (restates paragraph A), deleting the pointer (spec-citations.md requires a successor pointer) (spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **Hold must fail closed.** Do not copy `requireInjectionSupported`'s degrade-open on runtime not-found (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages.go ErrNotFound branch) into the hold decision. Resolve error or not-found sets hold=false (release) (spec.1.fix-G2.1, spec.2.fix-design-G1.1).
- **No "cannot be resolved" clause.** Do not add a separate unresolvable-runtime clause to paragraph A; "only when ... declares" plus "every other session" is the fail-closed statement. Do not re-add the predicate or a "checks injection too because..." defence to CODE-5 step 6 (spec.2.fix-G1.1, spec.2.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Withdrawn hold options.** Tried and rejected while paragraph A's predicate stands: a warm condition 5 on capability (holds a one_shot pod idle for nothing, second home); mapping one_shot to continuity "none" (changes the §7.1 field); letting the runtime refuse the second message (a delivered input, so a D 500 and failed session); multi_turn-only predicate (spec/code split); predicate by reference to the §5.1 injection gate (misses a one_shot runtime with injection true); a runtime registry in OpenResponsesOptions (new translator dependency) (spec.1.fix-G2.1, spec.2.fix-G1.1).
- **Two restatements of the predicate.** Summary decision 7 and non-spec docs edit (b) both restate paragraph A; both must change whenever the predicate changes. A prior fixer updated decision 7 and missed docs edit (b) (spec.2.follow-up-fix.1).
- **Tier-4 needs the chat runtime.** The echo reference runtime is `one_shot` (pkg/embedded/stack/catalog.go echoRuntime) and is now released after every turn. Held-session tier-4 tests must use the `chat` catalog entry (multi_turn, injection immediate) (spec.1.fix-G2.1).
- **Do not re-add §10.2 to paragraph B.** Do not write "may read under Section 10.2" in paragraph B unless CODE-4 stages an owner check and the adapter owner is no longer client-asserted. A citation without a check made the proposal contradict itself (spec.1.fix-G3.1 was corrected by spec.1.follow-up-fix.1) (spec.1.follow-up-fix.1).
- **Do not stage an owner check on adapter routes.** `principal.Subject == row.UserID` on these routes compares against the client-asserted `user`, so it would 404 legitimate callers and would be the only owner check on any route. The translator has no principal. It stays a Defects entry dependent on the owner fix (spec.1.fix-design-G3.1, spec.1.follow-up-fix.1).
- **Owner gap is settled; do not refile.** Tenant-scoped paragraph B plus the Defects entry is the settled state. The client-asserted owner also explains warm condition 4's user equality and the cold path's cross-user rehydrate; reviewers in rounds 2, 4, 6, and 8 marked this USEFUL and skipped refiling (USEFUL from spec.2.review-security.1, spec.4.review-fresh.1, spec.4.review-security.1, spec.6.review-security.1, spec.8 reviews).
- **Do not re-qualify paragraph D by delivery.** "after input reached", "after input may have reached", and "before or after ..." each left some dispatch failure with no rule (spec.1.fix-G4.1 and spec.4). Withdrawn: "a dispatch that paragraph C does not serve cold" (no "paragraph C" label in spec; reads as exempting a cold turn's own dispatch); importing SPEC-1's release via paragraph A (no 500, split home); enumerating pre-delivery root/cold cases; unconditional D (C and D both claim a warm pre-delivery failure) (spec.4.fix-G1.1, spec.4.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Do not phrase D's exclusion as "served cold".** A cold turn is served cold, so "a turn the continuation rules serve cold" exempts a cold turn's own dispatch failure. The exclusion names the failure C answers with a cold turn (spec.4.fix-G1.1, spec.4.fix-design-G1.1).
- **No "never refused" wording.** The cold path admits through §11.1 and can return the concurrency refusal; do not reintroduce "never refused" for continuations anywhere (spec.1.fix-G4.1).
- **Do not name executor errors in spec.** Naming ErrNotBound or SealedError in spec text puts code failure modes into spec (barred by the caller directive) (spec.1.fix-G4.1).
- **"Cold" excludes the root turn.** In this proposal "cold" means a continuation only (paragraph C); the root turn is neither warm nor cold. Any rule keyed on "warm or cold" omits it. Do not add a root-turn clause to paragraph B or warm condition 3; the capture point's home is paragraph D. Withdrawn: "and for a root turn" (drifting enumeration), "otherwise at session creation", redefining "cold" (spec.8.fix-G1.1, spec.8.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Do not re-key SPEC-3 rows on outcome.** A continuation that passes conditions 1-4 on the serving read but loses the reservation or has its dispatch refused at the coordinator is forwarded and served cold there; row 3's "serves the turn as §15 states" covers it. Do not restore row 3's "decides to forward after it reads the session row and before it writes the reservation" sentence (rule 1 restatement). Withdrawn: an exception clause on outcome selectors; restating row 2's selector in Edit A (spec.4.fix-G2.1, spec.4.fix-design-G2.1).
- **No request predicate in the §29.3 lead-in.** The lead-in after rule 8 enumerates every non-forward row with a one-clause "how served". Do not put "addresses no pre-existing session" or "has no holder" back; rounds 1-5 narrowed and widened it and each contradicted row 2's cold-continuation case. Withdrawn: "for a request that no held session serves"; dropping the adapter rows from the lead-in (spec.6.fix-G1.1, spec.6.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Edit A target wraps.** The locator "the built-in adapter row addresses no pre-existing session and so has no holder to forward to" wraps across two physical lines in spec/29 ("so has" / "no holder to forward to"); a single-line grep misses it. Grep "no holder to forward" or join lines (multiple reviews).
- **SPEC-4 bullet wraps.** The §15.1/§15.2 links sit on their own physical line in the hard-wrapped Messages bullet (spec.1.fix-design-G5.1).
- **Stale plan phrasing is not a gap.** The plan's "§6.2 idle timeout", "0056 transcript rehydration", and "cold path ... after the session ends" are stale; the proposal records 0059 and the idle-suspension reading (decision 3). Do not refile as conformance gaps (spec.1 and spec.4 plan-conformance).
- **Other plan items.** P2b "single-shot translators become ordinary terminal writers", F-15.1.54 (GET /v1/responses tenant-wide read), and F-15.1.62 belong to other plan items (spec.1 and spec.4 plan-conformance).
- **Refuted overlaps; do not refile.** SPEC-3 row 3 vs Forwarding rules 6/7, rows 1/2 503 overlap, and paragraph C's state list are refuted as one-clause applications (spec.4 and spec.8 single-source; spec.6.review-single-source.1).
- **Refused-warm-dispatch edge case.** Not filed: paragraph C plus "never withdrawn" plus warm condition 2 already state the outcome; completeness findings against the Edge cases commentary are refuted (spec.4.review-docs-alignment.1).
- **Superseded-RUNNING edge case.** The Edge cases section lists only the superseded-SUSPENDED case; the superseded-RUNNING case (fork, concurrent continuation, lost reservation, executor refusal) appears only in summary Non-goals and non-spec edge cases. Filed in spec.1; no ledger entry records its fix (spec.1.review-docs-alignment.1).
- **Pod failure mid warm turn.** §7.3 retry and paragraph D both write; terminal-from-any-non-terminal (0105) makes failed win at the cost of a wasted restore claim. Judged not a defect (spec.8.review-reliability.1).
- **Resources-only findings fail materiality.** §7.3 retry restoring held sessions onto unreachable pods, held-pod capacity, idle-suspend checkpoints never resumed, and the coordinator-crash 409 were judged resources-only or owner-settled (spec.1.review-performance.1, spec.8.review-performance.1).
- **SPEC-4 checklist drift.** Spec-loop reviewers treat SPEC-4's absence from the checklist as between-loop reconciliation, not a spec finding; see Deferred (spec.4, spec.6, spec.8 applicability).

### Open

- **Request-timeout outcome.** SPEC-1 names request timeout as a release trigger, but paragraph D does not; a held session timing out on the client deadline has no stated outcome (capacity, not correctness) — spec.1.review-reliability.1.
- **Pre-0108 response ids.** Bare session ids become malformed under paragraph B and answer 404 after upgrade; nothing states it — spec.1.review-docs-alignment.1.
- **Tier-1/2 pre-delivery failure case.** CODE-5's failure table and TEST-1 tier 1 lack a root or cold Send error before delivery (e.g. ErrNotBound on serveCold) asserting 500, failed, one release, no second cold attempt — spec.4.fix-G1.1, spec.4.fix-design-G1.1.
- **CODE-5 targets line.** Does not name `sessionSingleShotBinder` in credentialsurface.go (pre-existing) — spec.1.fix-G2.1.
- **DOCS-1(c) and TARGET_NOT_READY docs.** "The turn failed -> failed" may be unobservable; docs/reference/error-catalog.md and docs/client-guide/error-handling.md describe TARGET_NOT_READY as pre-running only — spec.8.review-docs-alignment.1.
- **UNVERIFIED: rule 4 vs admission-rate.** Whether the "admission-rate" creation gate counts as a §11.1 rate limit that Forwarding rule 4 tells the coordinator to skip; if so, a coordinator-side cold fallback contradicts rule 4 or skips the gate — spec.5.review-mechanism.1, spec.6.review-citations.1, spec.6.review-fresh.1, spec.6.review-mechanism.1.
- **UNVERIFIED: 0107 Impacts row.** spec.1.fix-G5.1 says no Impacts row is owed for SPEC-4; spec.2.review-applicability.1 says the summary Impacts table lacks a 0107 row although 0107 also edits spec/28 — spec.2.review-applicability.1.

### Deferred

- DEFERRED [proposals/0108_fix_proposal-0061-re-derived-plan-item-gateway-runtime-comms/0108_fix_proposal-0061-re-derived-plan-item-gateway-runtime-comms.implementation-checklist.md]: step S2 reads "SPEC-3. Split the §29.3 adapter off-holder row and edit the lead-in clause." No checklist step lands SPEC-4 (S1 names SPEC-1 and SPEC-2 only). The fix: S2 names "SPEC-3, SPEC-4" and adds "and add the §15 link to the §28.5.4 card's Messages bullet" (or a new spec step does). Still unclosed as of spec.8.review-applicability.1. (spec.1.fix-G5.1, spec.1.fix-design-G5.1)
- DEFERRED [summary.md]: The non-goal at line 77, "lets a session serve warm turns again after a post-restore cold turn re-seeds it", is false. A cold turn creates a new session S', so it re-seeds nothing on S; after a restore, S serves no warm turn again (only a warm reservation adds a response to S, and warm condition 3 blocks it), so the stated reason for preferring per-response generations over `recovery_generation == 0` does not hold. (spec.1.review-performance.1)
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: no staging touches pkg/gateway/externalapi/openapi/openapi.json; POST /v1/responses gains the 409 TARGET_NOT_READY (SPEC-3 row 3) and the post-delivery 500 (SPEC-2 paragraph D) responses. Whether to list them in the hand-authored document is for the non-spec loop to decide. (spec.4.review-client-surface.1)
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: in DOCS-1 (c), the row "The turn failed -> `failed`" contradicts SPEC-2 paragraph D. Paragraph D says a turn that fails answers a native `500` and leaves the response not completed, and GET answers only completed responses, so no returned response ever carries status `failed`. The docs loop owns this. (spec.6.review-docs-alignment.1)

### [spec.10.fix-G1.1]

DECISION: SPEC-3 row 2's route cell is the complement "Every `POST /v1/responses` that no forwarding row names", and its outcome reads "The serving replica serves the request itself as §15 **Open Responses conversation sessions.** states, and a session it creates for the request has the serving replica as its coordinating replica by construction". Edit A's clause reads "are served by the serving replica itself". BECAUSE the old predicate ("fails a warm condition") plus the restated outcome ("creates the session, claims a warm pod") sent a not-completed reserved response to session creation while SPEC-2 paragraphs B and C answer it 404, and left malformed or unknown ids in no row. A complement covers every POST /v1/responses by construction, and citing §15 keeps the 404 and creation outcomes in one home. ALTERNATIVES: "Every other POST /v1/responses ... rejects a previous_response_id that §15 answers with 404, and otherwise creates" (second home of the 404 rule); narrowing the predicate to "a response GET returns and that fails a warm condition" (malformed ids still in no row); reordering rows (renumbers "row 3" citers); a fourth rejected-continuation row (one more predicate).
WATCHOUT: Do not put a request predicate or a restated outcome back in SPEC-3 row 2. Rounds 4, 6, and 10 each keyed it on a predicate or restated "creates the session", and each version missed a §15 outcome (cold-continuation case, then the native 404 for not-completed, malformed, or unknown ids). Row 2 is the complement of row 3 and cites §15. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes SPEC-3 Edit B row 2; SPEC-2 paragraph B "a response that has not completed each return a native `404`".
DECISION: SPEC-3 row 3 ends "Rule 4 does not exempt a session the coordinator creates for the turn from the gates of §15 **Built-in adapter single-shot compute model.**" The design overview drops "governed by **Forwarding.** rules 1 through 8 as written" and summary decision 10 becomes "as SPEC-3 row 3 states". BECAUSE §11.1's control table lists "Rate limits (requests/min)" at per-runtime and per-pool granularity, which is the admission-rate creation gate, and rule 4 says the coordinator skips §11.1 rate limits on a forwarded request; in row 3 the serving replica creates nothing, so without the carve-out a coordinator-side cold fallback either contradicts rule 4 or skips the gate. ALTERNATIVES: editing rule 4 itself in spec/29 (widens to every forwarding row, where rule 4's reason holds); no staged change (§11.1 table names the limit); 409 instead of cold at the coordinator (changes paragraph C's outcome); serving replica applies the limit before forwarding (charges a warm turn, double-counts).
FACT: The tree already applies the admission-rate gate on a forwarded context: `CreateAndStartService` (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/service.go) replays `handleCreateAndStart` through a recorder, whose `createAndStartGates` calls `requireAdmissionRateLimit` (create_and_start.go:158) with no `Forwarded(ctx)` check. `Forwarded(ctx)` is read only in holder_forward.go:263 and mcptools. So the rule-4 carve-out needs no code change. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/service.go:234-245; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/create_and_start.go:118-160.
CORRECTS [Open: UNVERIFIED rule 4 vs admission-rate]: settled. The admission-rate gate is a §11.1 rate limit (spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:7 per-runtime, per-pool), so rule 4 did exempt it; SPEC-3 row 3 now states the carve-out. Move to Settled.
CORRECTS [Settled: Edit A wording]: Edit A now reads "the `POST /v1/chat/completions` row and the `POST /v1/responses` row that requires no forwarding are served by the serving replica itself". "Edit A picks one row" stays true.
CORRECTS [Settled: SPEC-3 row selection]: row 3 is selected by warm conditions 1-4 on the serving replica's read; row 2 is now its complement within POST /v1/responses rather than a second predicate.

The design asked this fixer to add, after the `sessionserver`: `ForwardResponsesTurn` bullet: "`sessionserver`: a create through `CreateAndStartService` whose request context carries `WithForwarded` answers `429 RATE_LIMITED` when the per-runtime admission limit is exhausted, as the same create without the mark does. `// spec: §29.3 (Interactive message send), §11.1`". ### [spec.10.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-3 row 2 is selected as the complement of row 3 ("Every `POST /v1/responses` that no forwarding row names") and its outcome cites §15 **Open Responses conversation sessions.** instead of restating "creates the session, claims a warm pod, dispatches" — BECAUSE every prior row-2 selector (outcome-keyed in round 4, warm-condition-keyed since) was a second predicate that missed a §15 outcome (here the native 404 for a not-completed, malformed, or unknown id); a complement cannot miss a request and a pointer cannot contradict §15 — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "rejects a previous_response_id that §15 answers with 404, and otherwise creates..." (restates paragraph C's 404 rule in the row, a second home); "naming a response GET returns and that fails a warm condition" (narrows the failed predicate again and still leaves malformed ids unnamed); reordering rows so the complement comes last (renames "row 3" everywhere, including the review log and edge case line).
DECISION: Edit A's clause becomes "are served by the serving replica itself" — BECAUSE the 404 continuation is served on no session, and the lead-in should not describe what the row owns.
DECISION: Forwarding rule 4 vs the coordinator's cold fallback is closed by one sentence at the end of row 3: "Rule 4 does not exempt a session the coordinator creates for the turn from the gates of §15 **Built-in adapter single-shot compute model.**" — BECAUSE row 3 is the only forwarding row whose coordinator can create a session, and row 3 already names its departures from the Forwarding rules — ALTERNATIVES: editing rule 4 in spec/29 (widens SPEC-3 for every forwarding row when rule 4's reason holds on all the others); no staged change (§11.1's control table lists "Rate limits (requests/min)" at per-runtime and per-pool granularity, so rule 4 literally exempts the admission-rate gate); having the coordinator answer 409 rather than serve cold (changes paragraph C's client outcome for races); having the serving replica pre-apply the per-runtime/per-pool limit (charges a warm turn that creates no session).
FACT: The tree's creation path applies the per-runtime/per-pool admission limit with no `Forwarded(ctx)` check, so a coordinator-side cold bind already passes it; only mcptools consults `Forwarded` — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/create_and_start.go:158; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/admission_ratelimit.go:27-42; pkg/gateway/middleware/ratelimit/ratelimit.go:13-18; grep "Forwarded(ctx)" hits only holder_forward.go and pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools.
FACT: §11.1's table calls the per-runtime/per-pool limit a rate limit ("Rate limits (requests/min) | Global, per-user, per-runtime, per-pool"), which settles the review log's "UNVERIFIED: rule 4 vs admission-rate": the contradiction was real — EVIDENCE: spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:7.
WATCHOUT: The `Forwarded` doc comment restates rule 4 without the row-3 exception ("the coordinator does not apply the §11.1 rate limits"); an implementor reading it could add a `Forwarded` skip to the creation path. The TEST-1 tier-1 bullet this design adds is the test that discriminates — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/holder_forward.go:241-247.
WATCHOUT: After this fix, "rules 1 through 8 as written/unchanged" is false; design line 9 and summary decision 10 now point at SPEC-3 row 3. Do not reintroduce a "governed by rules 1-8 as written" summary anywhere.

### [spec.12.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2 paragraph B's 404 sentence now reads "An id that names no completed response of a session in the caller's tenant returns a native `404`." — BECAUSE the old enumeration's "an ordinal the session has not reserved" covered the never-reserved ordinal 1 of every root and cold session (paragraph D captures their generation "at session creation", with no reservation), so GET and paragraph C would 404 S.1 and break every second turn; the complement of GET's success predicate cannot omit a recorded response — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "an ordinal the session row records no response for" (keeps a second statement of the success predicate as an enumeration; this sentence has been re-narrowed repeatedly); deleting the sentence (paragraph C and SPEC-3 cite "an id that GET would answer with 404", so the outcome needs one home).
WATCHOUT: Do not reintroduce an enumerated list of 404 cases in paragraph B, and do not edit CODE-4 step 6 or the handleGet test bullet to match: their code-level checks (unparsable, missing session, ordinal out of range, not completed) are how the code implements the complement, and malformed/unknown/cross-tenant ids remain 404 through it. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 step 6; spec-changes.md SPEC-2 paragraph B
FACT: Every "reserv" hit in the proposal outside paragraph B concerns warm-turn reservation (overview, paragraphs C and D, CODE-5, tests, summary decision 9 and rejected list) and stays true after this edit. Related-passages lookup judged 290 candidates, none dependent on the changed sentence.

### [spec.12.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2 paragraph B's 404 sentence becomes the complement of GET's success predicate: "An id that names no completed response of a session in the caller's tenant returns a native `404`." It replaces the four-case enumeration ("A malformed id, ... an ordinal the session has not reserved, and a response that has not completed each return a native `404`.") — BECAUSE "reserve" is defined only for warm turns (paragraph C), so the enumeration 404'd S.1 of every root and cold session and paragraph C then refused every second turn. A complement cannot miss or over-include a case and inherits any later change to the success predicate — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "an ordinal the session row records no response for" (closes it, but keeps an enumeration that is a second statement of the success predicate; same kind of answer that has been rewritten five times at this location); deleting the 404 sentence outright (paragraph C and SPEC-3 cite "an id GET would answer with 404", so the 404 must be stated somewhere).
WATCHOUT: Do not re-enumerate 404 cases in paragraph B. CODE-4 step 6's code-level list (unparsable, missing, out of range, not completed) and the handleGet test table are the implementation of the complement and stay as they are. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 step 6; non-spec-changes.md handleGet test bullet

### [spec.12.review-performance.1]
FACT: The round-11 diff (SPEC-3 Edit A "served by the serving replica itself", row 2 "Every `POST /v1/responses` that no forwarding row names", row 3 rule-4 sentence) changes no write path. A warm turn costs two conditional Postgres writes on the session row (reservation, completion) and no etcd write; the shipped per-request claim and release wrote pod/Sandbox state every call, so etcd write rate falls for held conversations. EVIDENCE: proposals/0108_.../0108_...spec-changes.md SPEC-2 paragraphs C and D; spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:572-576 (Forwarding rule 4).
FACT: Failure modes unchanged from the round-8 assessment: Postgres failover fails reservation and creation alike (503 or 500, no worse than the shipped creation path); Redis reset leaves REG-COORDLEASE on its Postgres fallback; a coordinator handoff yields row 3's bounded 409. No new hot key or watch. Nothing filed.
USEFUL [Standing context: Coordinator adoption, Transcript storage, Envelope growth]: settled the failover and growth questions without re-deriving.

### [f1.open-decisions.1]
DECISION: Open decision 1 (outcome of a held Open Responses turn that hits the request timeout) resolved as "end the session with the `failed` disposition and release its pod". Already staged, so no staged file was edited: a request timeout cancels `r.Context()`, on which the warm `Send` runs (pkg/gateway/environment/translator/open_responses.go:355; pkg/gateway/environment/translator/openai_chat.go:270-275 treats a request-context timeout as a failed dispatch), and CODE-5 step 5 answers every Send error other than the non-delivery refusals with `endHeldSession` and `500` (non-spec-changes.md:118, step 8 at :131). SPEC-2 paragraph D already ends the session `failed` when "a dispatch fails" (spec-changes.md:135-138). Summary non-goal "Leaving a failed warm session to the timers without ending it" (summary.md:80) rules out the alternative. Removed entry 1 from the summary's `## Open decisions for human to make`; entry 2 keeps its identifier. The optional paragraph D wording alignment was not written, because rung 1 held.
WATCHOUT: A timeout before the warm reservation writes nothing and leaves the held session `running` with no incomplete reservation; no turn started, so this decision does not cover it.

### [f1.open-decisions.2]
DECISION: Open decision 2 (response ids issued before this change) resolved as "accept the break". The staging already builds it: CODE-4 item 2 `parseResponseID` reports `ok == false` for a missing separator before any store call (non-spec-changes.md:94), and CODE-4 item 6 answers the native `404`. No tagged release exists (CHANGELOG.md:9; `git tag` lists no release), so no deployed id falls under the §15.5 support window (spec/15_external-api-surface.md, §15.5 item 1). Wrote one bullet stating the break under spec-changes.md `## Edge cases and accepted failure modes`, so an implementor adds no legacy-id fallback. Removed entry 2 from the summary's `## Open decisions for human to make`, which now reads "No decisions are open."

### [f1.other-proposals.0107]
DECISION: Added a 0107 row to the summary's `## Impacts on other proposals`, before the pinned-idle row. It records 0107 as Approved (proposals/0107_*/0107_*.status.md `approved-date: 2026-10-28`), with no deliverable losing its subject. The spec sections do not overlap: 0107 spec-changes.md:277,292 edit §28.5.2 and §29.4, and 0108 spec-changes.md:147,169 edit §29.3 and §28.5.4. `endHeldSession` (0108 non-spec-changes.md:130) runs no hook, which keeps 0107 non-spec-changes.md:75 true; `releaseSingleShot` (pkg/gateway/environment/translator/openai_chat.go:527-538) calls only `executor.ReleaseSession` and `sessionstore.RecordPodRelease`. The only coordination is a textual merge of the sessionstore edits (0107 CODE-8 at non-spec-changes.md:140, 0108 CODE-2 at non-spec-changes.md:45). No other summary text asserted anything about 0107. The ledger's UNVERIFIED 0107 Impacts row item (standing context line 113) is settled by this row.
WATCHOUT: If `endHeldSession` is changed to route through `OnSessionTerminal` or `AfterTerminalWrite`, 0107 CODE-3's exclusion sentence becomes false and this row must be rewritten.

### [non-spec-recheck.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: the native 409 for a refused warm forward is rendered by a `refuse func(w, currentState)` parameter of `ForwardResponsesTurn`, which `refuseForward` calls for `ForwardKindResponsesTurn` in place of `writeError`; CODE-5 step 3 returns on every non-Local outcome; CODE-3's old item 4 (forwardToHolder doc exception) is deleted — BECAUSE refuseForward is the only site in forwardToHolder that runs exclusively before any write (forwardTarget not-delivered, newForwardRequest failure, sendForward non-Delivered), while copyForwardResponse writes the status before copyBody can fail and return ForwardUnknown — ALTERNATIVES: copy-phase failure reported as ForwardDelivered (changes every route's rule-7 outcome and existing tests); a new ForwardRelayFailed outcome (new surface, keeps the caller-renders exception); a header-tracking ResponseWriter wrapper (must re-implement http.Flusher or streamBody stops flushing); sessionserver writing §15.1 envelope (SPEC-3 row 3 needs the native envelope).
FACT: forwardToHolder yields ForwardUnknown from sendForward (nothing written; refuseForward runs) and from copyForwardResponse after `w.WriteHeader(resp.StatusCode)` and partial copyBody (refuseForward never runs, DoNotStore set) — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/holder_forward.go forwardToHolder, copyForwardResponse.
WATCHOUT: do not make CODE-5 write the 409 "only on ForwardNotDelivered"; a sendForward-phase ForwardUnknown then reaches the client as an empty 200. Do not add a nil-refuse fallback either. — EVIDENCE: holder_forward.go sendForward returns ForwardUnknown before any write.
WATCHOUT: the tree's `Forwarded` doc comment says the coordinator "does not apply the §11.1 rate limits"; that refers to the forwarded request's own route, and does not exempt a session the coordinator creates for the turn (SPEC-3 row 3). The new TEST-1 bullet (`CreateAndStartService` under `WithForwarded` refused with RATE_LIMITED) pins it; a Forwarded skip added to createAndStartGates fails it. CreateAndStartService runs handleCreateAndStart on the caller's ctx, so the test is tier 1. — EVIDENCE: holder_forward.go Forwarded doc; create_and_start.go requireAdmissionRateLimit call; service.go CreateAndStartService.
OPEN: §29.3 Forwarding rule 7 and the §28.8 CH-HOLDERFORWARD "Transport fails mid-stream" cell promise a 409 for an unknown outcome on every route, which copyForwardResponse cannot keep once the coordinator's status is relayed. Pre-existing, owed to a spec proposal on rule 7 (mid-relay carve-out); SPEC-3 row 3 and spec-changes consequence bullet inherit it. Candidate for the summary's Defects list in a later round.
CORRECTS [reconcile.after-spec.1 OPEN on TEST-1 rule-4 carve-out]: closed; TEST-1 Tier 1 now has the admission bullet.

### [non-spec-recheck.1.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: Warm condition 4 compares request with request. SPEC-2 condition 4 now reads "This request asks for the user, runtime, and environment that the request which created `S` asked for." CODE-2 adds `Session.RequestedRuntimeRef` on the request-envelope bundle, the serveCold commit (CODE-5 step 6) writes it beside ContinuationParentID, and CODE-5 step 2 compares req.User/row.UserID, environment/row.Environment, runtime ref/row.RequestedRuntimeRef — BECAUSE row.RuntimeRef is rewritten at creation by the low-priority PreRoute chain, by the §10.7 experiment router, and by the priority>=300 PreRoute MODIFY, so comparing with it sends every experiment-routed continuation cold and leaves one held pod per turn — ALTERNATIVES: RequestedEnvironment field (Environment is never rewritten; duplicate); raw `model` string (empty vs explicit default mismatch); write at creation via SingleShotSpec (new create field; cold commit is already atomic with ordinal 1); predict routing on the warm turn (nondeterministic, runs interceptors); drop runtime (model switch served warm on old runtime); exception on ExperimentContext (misses PreRoute rewrites).
FACT: Session creation rewrites RuntimeRef in three places and copies UserID and Environment unchanged — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/create_and_start.go:284-308 (runtimeRef := req.RuntimeRef, then PreRoute; Environment: req.Environment), :365-366 (afterRoute MODIFY), pkg/gateway/sessionserver/experimentrouter.go:248-250.
FACT: The translator's requested runtime ref is splitEnvModel(req.Model) defaulted to h.defaultRuntime — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/environment/translator/open_responses.go:291-294; CreateAndStartService rejects an empty RuntimeRef (create_and_start.go:201), so a created row's RequestedRuntimeRef is never empty and an empty stored value fails condition 4 (fail-closed).
WATCHOUT: The step 5 reservation re-check cites "conditions 1 through 4" and inherits step 2's field mapping; do not restate the mapping there, and do not add RequestedRuntimeRef to SPEC-2 paragraph B's row-records sentence (not exhaustive; condition 4 already holds "on S's row") — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-5 step 5; spec-changes.md SPEC-2 paragraph B.
WATCHOUT: The binder's hold decision runtime ref (spec.RuntimeRef vs the created row's RuntimeRef) is a separate finding and was deliberately not touched here.
FACT: Citer sweep beyond the design's siteDispositions also changed the CODE-2 heading, checklist S5, and summary Overview "Stores" bullet, each of which described CODE-2's bundle additions as per-response records only.

### [non-spec-recheck.1.fix-G4.1]
DECISION: endHeldSession is a claim writer that runs the post-terminal hook inline (BeginTerminal, failed Update with ErrSuperseded on a terminal row, AfterTerminalWrite with nil signals), through translator consumer interface `SessionTerminal` (method set of mcptools.Terminal) that *sessionserver.Server satisfies directly; it calls no releaseSingleShot — BECAUSE only the hook body runs the §7.2 inbox drain / §7.3 DLQ drain and the terminal events (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go afterTerminalWrite: drainMessagingOnTerminal, emitStatusChange, emitSessionComplete, releaseAfterTerminal), and releaseSingleShot calls only executor.ReleaseSession and RecordPodRelease (openai_chat.go releaseSingleShot). The `lenny/cancel_session` writer (mcptools_register.go cancelSession) is the exact precedent — ALTERNATIVES: OnSessionTerminal via a cmd adapter (0107 CODE-3 makes it detached, so release would follow the response, against SPEC-2 paragraph D); ForceTerminate (stamps FORCE_TERMINATED); ReportSessionFailure (§7.3 may retry/resume); a drain call inside releaseSingleShot (partial parallel terminal pipeline).
DECISION: serveCold has one failure exit: every failure after BindSingleShot returns, a Send error included, calls endHeldSession and answers 500; a non-held success writes `completed` and calls releaseSingleShot directly. The deferred failed-disposition release and its disarm are gone — BECAUSE once post-Send cold failures run the hook, the pre-/post-Send split has no basis and kept a second (hookless failed) writer kind in serveCold.
WATCHOUT: Withdrawn option: the two-release-owner cold path (deferred releaseSingleShot owning serveCold Send errors, endHeldSession owning later failures, disarm first). It lost because it left a hookless `failed` write that skips the inbox/DLQ drain, needed disarm bookkeeping, and the summary watch-out existed only to police it. Do not restore a deferred release in serveCold. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes CODE-5 step 7.
WATCHOUT: The release-timeout-bounded context endHeldSession uses bounds only its Update. AfterTerminalWrite re-derives its own context with context.WithoutCancel plus the terminal pipeline budget, so it is not cut by the release timeout (§7.1 Terminal pipeline context). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go terminalContext.
FACT: The terminal-writer gate is two coupled tables: terminalWriterInventory (terminal_writer_inventory_test.go) and terminalContextWriters (terminal_context_internal_test.go), cross-checked through TerminalContextWriterKeys. A writer outside sessionserver that runs AfterTerminalWrite is driven by afterTerminalWriteDriver (as cancelNode and cancelSession are); the translators' rows today are none-path (`noHook`). — EVIDENCE: terminal_writer_inventory_test.go terminalWriterInventory, terminal_context_internal_test.go terminalContextWriters.
FACT: 0106 edits the inventory files in CODE-B (writeTerminalCallers, writeTerminal seed row), CODE-C (expireOrphan -> endOrphan), and CODE-E (startReconcilerWorkers -> deadlockFail), not CODE-A as the design guessed. 0107 CODE-2 edits terminal_writer_inventory_test.go; 0107 CODE-3 lists the translators' handleCreate as hookless and has a `lenny/cancel_session` inline-AfterTerminalWrite row that endHeldSession matches. — EVIDENCE: 0106 non-spec-changes.md CODE-B/CODE-C/CODE-E; 0107 non-spec-changes.md CODE-2 Targets, CODE-3 hook-dispatch table.
CORRECTS [design G4 0106 row labels]: the design's proposed "0106 CODE-A and CODE-C" is wrong; the Impacts row names CODE-B, CODE-C, and CODE-E.
OPEN: The standing-context Open item "Tier-1/2 pre-delivery failure case" (root or cold Send error before delivery) is now covered by TEST-1 tier 1's Failure exit table, which lists a serveCold `Send` error; a later compaction can retire it.

### [non-spec-recheck.1.fix-G5.1]

DECISION: Added a **Tier 8 (chaos).** block to TEST-1, between Tier 7a and Tier 9, covering a coordinator crash mid warm turn, a restore between turns, a restore mid-turn, and a coordinator crash after a forwarded read. Added 8 to S10 and to summary TEST-1, and added `tests/tier8_chaos` and the lifecycleharness mux route to the TEST-1 Targets. BECAUSE test-coverage.md requires tier 8 for a failure or recovery path, and the only analogue (tests/tier8_chaos/holder_forward_chaos_test.go TestCoordinatorCrashAfterReadingAForwardIsRefusedAndNotHeld_spec_29_3) covers ForwardKindMessage only. ALTERNATIVES: a single sentence (the cases need distinct injection points); pointing at tier-1 fakes (no real failure injection).

It does not say "as cmd/lenny-gateway wires them", which is what the design wrote. BECAUSE `sessionSingleShotBinder` and the HolderForwarder adapter live in package main (cmd/lenny-gateway/credentialsurface.go:231-259), so the harness cannot import them. ALTERNATIVES: moving the adapters to pkg/ (scope growth, not required by the finding).
DECISION: The design's "and a `// diagnosis:` comment" was dropped from the block, because the Testing preamble already requires it for every tier-2-and-up test.
FACT: lifecycleharness gives Crash (harness.go:615), SetForwardFault (harness.go:598, runs before the handler chain, so a faulted forward never reaches handleCreate), SuspendIdle (suspension.go:50), RunToQuiescence and Check (checker.go), and the package-level `Report(t, r, id, reason)` (cells.go:38). A `runtime_crash` report with a spare pod restores the session to running with RecoveryGeneration >= 1 (cells.go:344-352). serveReplica's inner mux routes only /mcp and / → r.Server.Handler() (harness.go:566-575). The harness runtime is "echo" (cluster.go:45). — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/lifecycleharness/
WATCHOUT: The tier-8 harness binder has to return `hold = true` itself. The harness runtime "echo" declares no multi_turn, so any binder that resolves capabilities releases every session, and the chaos cases then silently test release. — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/lifecycleharness/cluster.go:45, harness.go:408-414

### [non-spec-recheck.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: ForwardResponsesTurn takes a `refuse func(http.ResponseWriter, currentState string)` that refuseForward calls for ForwardKindResponsesTurn in place of writeError, after DoNotStore. CODE-5 then returns on every outcome other than ForwardLocal, which is the contract every other forwardToHolder caller already follows, and CODE-3 item 4 (the caller-renders exception in the forwardToHolder doc) is deleted — BECAUSE refuseForward runs only in the pre-write phases (forwardTarget not-delivered, newForwardRequest failure, sendForward failure), so it is the one site that knows the response is still unwritten — ALTERNATIVES: report a copy-phase failure as ForwardDelivered by changing copyForwardResponse (breaks rule-7 semantics and existing holder_forward_test.go expectations for every kind); a new exported ForwardOutcome value (new surface on every forwardToHolder caller); a header-tracking ResponseWriter wrapper checked by CODE-5 (has to re-implement http.Flusher for streamBody's `w.(http.Flusher)` assertion, and adds a conditional at the caller).
FACT: forwardToHolder returns ForwardUnknown from two phases: sendForward after GotConn (nothing written, refuseForward writes 409) and copyForwardResponse after w.WriteHeader(resp.StatusCode) when copyBody fails (no refusal; DoNotStore only). Every existing caller returns at once on a non-Local outcome, so the copy-phase truncation is the shipped behavior on every route. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/holder_forward.go:298-325, :439-462; messages_delivery.go:128-136
OPEN: §29.3 Forwarding rule 7 (spec/29 "A request whose forward outcome is unknown fails closed with `TARGET_NOT_READY`, HTTP 409 ... whatever its route") and the spec/28 off-holder matrix "Transport fails mid-stream" cell promise a 409 after the coordinator's status may already be relayed, which copyForwardResponse cannot send on any route. SPEC-3 row 3 ("When the forward is not delivered or its outcome is unknown ... fails closed with `TARGET_NOT_READY`") and the spec-changes consequence bullet inherit it. Pre-existing; not caused by this fix. Per the caller directive it belongs in the summary's "Defects in the shipped tree that this proposal does not stage" list, e.g. "**Rule 7 after a relayed status.** §29.3 Forwarding rule 7 and the §28 off-holder matrix answer an unknown outcome with 409, but a coordinator body that fails after its status was relayed leaves the truncated response kept out of §11.5 (copyForwardResponse in pkg/gateway/sessionserver/holder_forward.go) on every route." A reconcile pass decides whether to add it.
WATCHOUT: TEST-1's translator bullet must include a stub that writes a 200 status and returns ForwardUnknown without calling refuse; that is the assertion that discriminates the copy-phase case. A stub that returns ForwardUnknown and writes nothing passes against the old design too. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md TEST-1 "Forwarding §29.3 rule 1" bullet
FACT: CreateAndStartService builds an httptest request on the caller's ctx and runs handleCreateAndStart, whose createAndStartGates calls requireAdmissionRateLimit with no Forwarded check, so a WithForwarded ctx reaches the §11.1 admission gate. A tier-1 sessionserver test of the rule-4 carve-out is buildable today. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/service.go:234-246; create_and_start.go:118,158; admission_ratelimit.go:42-60
WATCHOUT: the tree's `Forwarded` doc comment says the coordinator "does not apply the §11.1 rate limits" to a forwarded request. That is true of the request-scoped middleware and false of the session-creation admission gate. The new TEST-1 bullet pins the gate. This design does not rewrite the comment. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/holder_forward.go:241-250
CORRECTS [reconcile.after-spec.1 OPEN on TEST-1 rule-4 carve-out]: closed by one TEST-1 Tier 1 bullet asserting CreateAndStartService under WithForwarded is refused RATE_LIMITED once the per-runtime limit is exhausted. The second half of the spec lane's suggested bullet (an end-to-end forwarded cold turn) is dropped, because it re-tests the same gate through more layers.

### [non-spec-recheck.1.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: Warm condition 4 compares what the incoming request asks for with what the request that created S asked for. SPEC-2 condition 4 is replaced in place ("This request asks for the user, runtime, and environment that the request which created `S` asked for."). CODE-2 adds one bundle field `Session.RequestedRuntimeRef` (json `requestedRuntimeRef,omitempty`, same storedEnvelope/requestEnvelopeArg/NULL-check/applyStoredEnvelope plumbing as `Responses`). The CODE-5 serveCold commit writes it beside `ContinuationParentID` (root and cold alike). CODE-5 step 2 compares user with `row.UserID`, environment with `row.Environment`, and runtime ref with `row.RequestedRuntimeRef`, and never with `row.RuntimeRef`. BECAUSE `row.RuntimeRef` is rewritten at creation by the low-priority PreRoute chain, by §10.7 `routeExperiment`, and by a priority>=300 PreRoute MODIFY, while `UserID` and `Environment` are copied from the request unchanged. The old spec wording "those the request would create its own session with" also cannot be evaluated under non-sticky routing or a per-request interceptor, because it asks what a hypothetical creation would do. ALTERNATIVES: record both runtime and environment (environment is never rewritten, so a second field adds nothing); record the raw `model` string (a request that leaves `model` empty and one that names the default runtime would mismatch); write the field at creation through SingleShotSpec/CreateSessionRequest (new sessionserver create field, and the commit already writes ordinal 1 atomically, so no continuation can see the row before the field exists); re-run routing to predict the runtime (nondeterministic, and it runs interceptors on a warm turn); drop runtime from condition 4 (a client that switches model mid-conversation would be served warm on the old runtime).
FACT: Session creation rewrites RuntimeRef in three places: create_and_start.go `runtimeRef := req.RuntimeRef; if preRoute.RequestedRuntime != ""` (PreRoute below priority 300), `routeExperiment` -> experimentrouter.go `row.RuntimeRef = variant.Runtime`, and create_and_start.go `afterRoute.RequestedRuntime != row.RuntimeRef`. UserID and Environment come from req unchanged (create_and_start.go row literal `Environment: req.Environment`). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/create_and_start.go:284-287,306-308,345-374; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/experimentrouter.go:248-250
FACT: The translator's runtime ref is `splitEnvModel(req.Model)` with `h.defaultRuntime` as fallback, passed to `BindSingleShot` as `SingleShotSpec.RuntimeRef`. This is the value both the commit records and step 2 computes for the incoming request. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/environment/translator/open_responses.go:288-311
WATCHOUT: The binder's hold decision (CODE-5 step 6, `sessionSingleShotBinder`) has to resolve capabilities on the EFFECTIVE runtime (`resp.RuntimeRef` from `CreateAndStartService`, which `toResponse` fills from `row.RuntimeRef`) rather than `spec.RuntimeRef`. Otherwise an experiment-routed session is held or released according to the capabilities of the runtime the client named, which is not the variant it actually runs. CODE-5 step 6 does not name which ref, so this is a separate finding that this fix neither causes nor repairs. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/credentialsurface.go:259-268; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_response.go:169-203
OPEN: File the binder-runtime-ref ambiguity above as a non-spec finding against CODE-5 step 6 in a later round.

### [non-spec-recheck.1.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: endHeldSession becomes an OpenResponsesHandler method in open_responses.go that opens Terminal.BeginTerminal, writes `failed` (MarkTerminated; ErrSuperseded on a terminal row) and runs Terminal.AfterTerminalWrite(ctx, from, updated, nil) inline; it calls no releaseSingleShot. Terminal is a translator consumer interface with mcptools.Terminal's method set, satisfied by *sessionserver.Server directly (no cmd adapter) — BECAUSE only the post-terminal hook runs the §7.2/§7.3 inbox and DLQ drain and the terminal events, and lenny/cancel_session (mcptools_register.go cancelSession) is the exact precedent — ALTERNATIVES: OnSessionTerminal through a cmd adapter like budgetSessionTerminator (0107 CODE-3 rule 1 makes OnSessionTerminal detached, so the release would follow the response, against paragraph D); sessionAdminAdapter.ForceTerminate (labels the row FORCE_TERMINATED and is also OnSessionTerminal); ReportSessionFailure (§7.3 classifier may retry, against paragraph D).
DECISION: serveCold gets one failure exit: every failure after BindSingleShot returns, including a Send error, calls endHeldSession; the deferred failed-disposition releaseSingleShot and its disarm are deleted; a non-held success writes `completed` and calls releaseSingleShot directly — BECAUSE the staged pre-Send/post-Send owner split had no principled basis once post-Send cold failures run the hook, and it removes CODE-5 step 7's disarm dance and the summary's two-release-owners watch-out — ALTERNATIVES: keep the deferred release for cold Send errors (two failure exits, a hookless failed write in serveCold, step 7 and the watch-out stay).
FACT: two coupled tier-1 tables pin terminal writers: terminalWriterInventory (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_writer_inventory_test.go:59-60, compare and "A new terminal writer needs an inventory row" near :262-274) and terminalContextWriters (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_context_internal_test.go:286-287), cross-checked through TerminalContextWriterKeys (:296, used at terminal_writer_inventory_test.go:345). A writer key is "file funcName"; a claim writer must call BeginTerminal before its first Update in the claim function. — EVIDENCE: terminal_writer_inventory_test.go:300-314
FACT: 0106 (approved, unimplemented here) also edits both inventory files (0106 non-spec-changes.md:55-56 and :101), and the 0108 summary Impacts table has no 0106 row. — EVIDENCE: proposals/0106_*/0106_*.non-spec-changes.md:55,101
FACT: today neither translator writes a `failed` row on a Send error; the deferred releaseSingleShot releases the pod and the row stays `running` (open_responses.go handleCreate Send-error branch; openai_chat.go handleCreateCompletion Send-error branch). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/environment/translator/openai_chat.go:290-295
OPEN: whether a Chat Completions session left `running` with no pod after a dispatch failure is a shipped-tree defect for the Defects list (SPEC-1's "recording the failed §6.2 terminal disposition" may mean the pod disposition only). Nobody has checked what ends that row.
WATCHOUT: 0107 CODE-3 line 75 names the translators' `handleCreate` as a hookless writer and its Hook dispatch table has no translator row; after 0108 the open_responses.go hookless writer is serveCold and endHeldSession is an inline AfterTerminalWrite writer like the lenny/cancel_session row. ### [non-spec-recheck.2.review-mechanism.1]

FACT: `Server.AfterTerminalWrite` derives its context through `terminalContext`, which applies `context.WithoutCancel` to the context it receives. That strips the caller's deadline as well as its cancellation, so the release-timeout bound that CODE-5 step 8 puts on `endHeldSession`'s context does not cut the hook's seal, drain, or release. The terminal pipeline budget alone bounds them. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:2027-2033, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go:77-83
FACT: The hook's release step releases through the sessionserver's executor, which is the same `w.exec` the translator holds. It records `PodReleasedAt` only when the binding sits in the replica's registry, and `CreateAndStartService` registers it there. So `endHeldSession` ending in `AfterTerminalWrite` releases the pod and records the release, matching what `releaseSingleShot` did. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/sessionsrv.go:183, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go:173-195, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/service.go:208-216
FACT: `copyForwardResponse` writes the coordinator's status before it copies the body and returns ForwardUnknown when the body fails. Every `refuseForward` call site runs before any coordinator byte is written. So the doc comment's claim that `refuse` is called only before any byte is written matches the tree. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/holder_forward.go:298-319, 439-461
FACT: The per-runtime and per-pool §11.1 admission-rate checks run inside `handleCreateAndStart` (`requireAdmissionRateLimit`), and nothing there checks `Forwarded(ctx)`. The tier-1 `CreateAndStartService`+`WithForwarded` RATE_LIMITED test therefore pins existing behavior. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/create_and_start.go:158
WATCHOUT: CODE-5 step 8's parenthetical reason for "never `OnSessionTerminal`" is loose. `OnSessionTerminal` (via `recordSessionCompleted`) also releases inline. The choice of `AfterTerminalWrite` with nil signals matches the `lenny/cancel_session` pattern and is sound, so I did not file it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:255-257, 331-339; pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:486-517

### [non-spec-recheck.4.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode findings on the r2->r4 delta (TEST-1 tier-1 "Failure exit" superseded-branch sentence; tier-7a checker adds concurrent terminates and "no session emitted more than one session_complete") — BECAUSE the delta is test text only and adds no store write, watch, or lock; the per-warm-turn cost stays two session-row Updates plus one transcript append, with no etcd or CRD write — ALTERNATIVES: re-filing the Postgres-failover leak (see WATCHOUT) was rejected.
WATCHOUT: endHeldSession releases only on an applied Update (CODE-5 step 8), so during a Postgres failover (15-30s, spec/04 §4.4.5 "Postgres fallback retry during failover") a turn whose record or commit fails also fails the terminal write, and its pod, lease, and §11.1 slot stay held until the 900s maxClientIdleSeconds bound (spec/11 §11.3 timeout table). Shipped handleCreate released through the executor regardless of the store (pkg/gateway/environment/translator/openai_chat.go releaseSingleShot). This deviates from paragraph D's "releasing its pod and lease before the response returns" only in the store-down case. A held session already keeps its pod up to 900s after its last turn when the client abandons it, so the extra cost lands only on hold=false sessions. Not filed, following the owner's GC rule and non-spec-recheck reliability decision log line 599. Do not re-derive this. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-5 step 8; review-log.md:599
USEFUL [standing context "Envelope growth", "Coordinator adoption", "Transcript storage"]: settled the jsonb-growth, Redis-reset, and handoff questions without re-deriving them.

### [non-spec-recheck.4.review-reliability.1]
FACT: Every `running` session has an idle reclaimer: §6.2 idle-suspends it or, for `executionMode: service` and `type: mcp`, expires it at `maxClientIdleSeconds`. So a held or unheld adapter session left `running` because `endHeldSession`'s Update hit a store error (for example during a Postgres failover) is reclaimed by the timers. This is bounded GC and is not a leak finding. The watchdog has no single-shot exclusion. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:291 "For such a session the clock fires the `expired` transition"; grep of pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog for SingleShot returns nothing.
FACT: In the round-3 delta (the endHeldSession ErrSuperseded test), the concurrent terminator runs `AfterTerminalWrite`, which releases the local executor binding (`releaseAfterTerminal`). A hookless terminal writer leaves the release to the coordination Sweeper once the claim ends. In both cases "no AfterTerminalWrite from endHeldSession" is correct. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go:14-33, 77-120; pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/terminal_mark.go:20.
DECISION: no findings in recheck round 4 under reliability — BECAUSE the delta (TEST-1 failure-exit superseded case, tier-7a terminate injection and invariants) matches CODE-5 step 8 and the hook contract, and every recovery path traced (crash after the reservation, ForwardUnknown retry, adoption ErrNotBound, generation advance) either goes cold or ends under existing timers — ALTERNATIVES: filing "the 500 may return before the other writer's hook releases the pod" was rejected as wording-level, because the session was already ended by its own writer.

The concurrent writer's own hook releases the pod when endHeldSession's write is superseded — ALTERNATIVES: filing nil signals in endHeldSession's AfterTerminalWrite as a dropped audit surface was rejected. ### [spec.14.review-reliability.1]
FACT: Round-13 diff to spec-changes is limited to warm condition 4 (now compares the request with what S's creating request asked for), paragraph B recording the creating request's runtime, the collapsed 404 sentence, and one new edge-case bullet. None of these changes the reservation CAS, the recovery_generation fencing of the completion write, or the cold fallback. Condition 4 compares immutable recorded fields, so the reservation's "while conditions 1 through 4 still hold" stays a pure row CAS. EVIDENCE: proposals/0108_.../0108_...spec-changes.md:102-111
DECISION: no reliability finding this round. BECAUSE I traced each failure in turn and every one leaves a not-completed newest response, so warm condition 2 sends every later continuation cold and the §6.2 timers reclaim the pod. The traced failures were: a crash after the reservation, after the completion write, or mid forward (outcome unknown, then 409 and the retry goes cold); a stale coordinator completing a response after a handoff (harmless, because the turn did run); a Postgres failover during the completion write (D answers 500); and an eviction during a warm turn (D ends S failed, racing §7.3 restore, and either order leaves a consistent row). ALTERNATIVES: filing "D ends a session the §7.3 retry is restoring". I rejected it because it is a deliberate fail-closed choice and a restored S can never be served warm again (condition 3).

### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: §29.3 rule 5 for a forwarded continuation that fails a warm condition is closed by moving forwardTarget's Forwarded(ctx) branch ahead of forwardGated for every kind (CODE-3 item 1), and CODE-5 step 3 now calls ForwardResponsesTurn on every forwarded request whatever its warm verdict; ForwardLocal then routes to step 5 (warm-eligible) or step 6 (holder's cold turn, SPEC-3 row 3) — BECAUSE rule 5 is unconditional and the tree tests the state gate first, so a forwarded request on a non-running row ran locally at a non-holder — ALTERNATIVES: a lease pre-check inside the ForwardResponsesTurn wrapper (second copy of rule 5, leaves other kinds open); gating ForwardKindResponsesTurn on every state (breaks forwardStates' meaning); a translator-side lease check (new consumer-interface method); a SPEC-2/SPEC-3 exception (not needed, spec-changes untouched).
FACT: forwardTarget returns ForwardLocal on !forwardGated before the Forwarded(ctx)/coordinatesSession branch; coordinatesSession returns true under coordinatesEverySession, so the reorder keeps the single-gateway posture local — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/holder_forward.go forwardTarget; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/coordination_lease.go coordinatesSession.
WATCHOUT: the reorder changes every kind. A forwarded MessageNoReplyTo at a non-holder on a row outside messageForwardStates now gets ForwardNotDelivered, and forwardMessageBatch then holds it in the DLQ (holdUndeliveredForward). §29.3 rule 6 limits the DLQ hold to a `running` or `input_required` session and gives every other request 409. Before the reorder the same request ran locally at the non-holder, which is a rule-5 violation, so the reorder is no regression — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go forwardMessageBatch; spec/29 §29.3 Forwarding rule 6.
OPEN: whether that forwarded no-inReplyTo message on a non-running row at a non-holder should get the 409 of rule 6's last sentence instead of the DLQ hold. That is a pre-existing tree refinement outside this finding, and a later round should decide whether it belongs in the summary's Defects list.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: endHeldSession falls back to releaseSingleShot(executor.DispositionFailed) when its own Update returns any error other than sessionstore.ErrSuperseded (CODE-5 step 8), with one TEST-1 tier-1 "Failure exit" case (Update fails from the commit onward, warm and cold: 500, exactly one executor release with DispositionFailed, zero AfterTerminalWrite calls) — BECAUSE no hook runs when no write applied, so a Postgres failover otherwise strands pod and §4.9 lease until maxClientIdleSeconds, breaking SPEC-2 paragraph D; ErrSuperseded stays excluded because the concurrent terminal writer's hook releases — ALTERNATIVES: leave to the idle watchdog (breaks D, regresses from shipped deferred release); restore a deferred releaseSingleShot in serveCold/serveWarm (double release on an applied write); retry the Update (new timer loop); narrow to "store error" (ErrNotFound and deadline would then strand, not fail-closed).
FACT: releaseSingleShot detaches its context (context.WithoutCancel plus rel.timeout) and RecordPodRelease records nothing on a non-terminal row, so the fallback on a still-running row releases the executor and leaves the row for the watchdog, which is safe because continuations of a released session go cold. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/environment/translator/openai_chat.go releaseSingleShot (doc comment "A row that is not terminal ... records nothing"); executor.DispositionFailed at pkg/gateway/session/executor/executor.go:206.
WATCHOUT: CODE-5 step 7's "endHeldSession is the only release on a failed exit" stays true because the fallback sits inside endHeldSession; do not reword it to "releaser", and do not add a second release site in the handlers. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-5 steps 7 and 8.
CORRECTS [ledger spec-round DECISION at review-log :388 and :432 "it calls no releaseSingleShot"]: true only for an applied write or ErrSuperseded; any other Update error now calls releaseSingleShot with DispositionFailed.
