### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]

DECISION: close the rule-5 gap by moving the `Forwarded(ctx)` branch in `forwardTarget` ahead of `forwardGated` for every kind (one sentence appended to CODE-3 item 1), and by having CODE-5 step 3 call `ForwardResponsesTurn` on every forwarded request whatever its warm verdict (step 2 sends only unforwarded failures straight to step 6; on ForwardLocal step 3 continues to step 5 or 6) — BECAUSE rule 5 is unconditional, and the reorder keeps one rule-5 check in the code — ALTERNATIVES: a lease pre-check inside the `ForwardResponsesTurn` wrapper (a second copy of the rule-5 branch, which is an exception clause); listing every state in `forwardStates[ForwardKindResponsesTurn]` (breaks the map's documented meaning); a translator-side lease check (no consumer interface reaches the lease).
FACT: `forwardTarget` tests `forwardGated` before `Forwarded(ctx)`, so a forwarded request of any kind whose row at the receiver is outside the kind's forward states answers ForwardLocal and runs at a non-holder. No existing test pins that order: the only WithForwarded test, TestForwardedRequestIsNeverForwardedAgain_spec_29_3, uses a running row. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/holder_forward.go:259-269; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/holder_forward_test.go:259-284
FACT: `coordinatesSession` returns true under `coordinatesEverySession()`, so the reorder keeps the single-gateway posture local. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/coordination_lease.go:98-118
WATCHOUT: the reorder also changes the existing kinds (terminate, message, interrupt, upload, resume). A forwarded request that reaches a non-holder whose row has left the kind's forward states now gets ForwardNotDelivered (409, or the caller holds a no-inReplyTo message) where it used to run locally. Rule 5 requires that outcome. The Impacts 0101 row and checklist S6 carry the change. No other proposal names forwardTarget. — EVIDENCE: grep forwardTarget proposals/
WATCHOUT: SPEC-2 paragraph C "Every other continuation is served cold" and the SPEC-3 catch-all row are not falsified by a rule-5 409. Paragraph C defers the off-replica case to §29.3, rule 5 governs every forwarding row, and row 3 (selected on the serving replica's read) already allows a cold turn at the holder ("a session the coordinator creates for the turn"). Do not stage a spec exception for this. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-2 paragraph C last sentence; SPEC-3 row 3; spec/29_communication-scenarios.md §29.3 Forwarding rule 5
OPEN: a forwarded request whose head row was erased between the serving replica's read and the receiver's read answers 404 at CODE-5 step 1, before step 3 can check the lease. That answer has no effect, but rule 5 literally prescribes the rule-6 409. Is this worth a rule? It is not staged.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: endHeldSession gains one fallback: when its `failed` Update returns any error other than `sessionstore.ErrSuperseded`, it calls `releaseSingleShot` with `executor.DispositionFailed`. It goes in the CODE-5 step 8 sentence "The hook releases the pod and lease, so `endHeldSession` calls no `releaseSingleShot`.", scoped to an applied write, and TEST-1's Tier 1 "Failure exit" bullet gets one appended case — BECAUSE SPEC-2 paragraph D already requires release before the response, and the shipped handler releases unconditionally through `defer releaseSingleShot` (open_responses.go handleCreate), so the fallback restores parity with no spec edit and no new mechanism — ALTERNATIVES: leave the row and pod to the idle watchdog (rejected: it strands the pod and §4.9 lease for maxClientIdleSeconds, 900s by default, and breaks paragraph D); a deferred releaseSingleShot in the handler beside endHeldSession (rejected: it makes two releasers on the applied-write path, which step 7 rules out); retrying endHeldSession's write (rejected: it adds a timer and loop for a failover case).
FACT: releasing the pod while the row stays `running` is safe. On the warm path the reservation leaves ordinal n+1 `Completed: false`, so every continuation goes cold (n < len, or the head is not completed). On the cold path a failed commit leaves no ordinal 1, so nothing can address the session. A later watchdog termination runs the hook against a released pod. The shipped handler leaves the same row state when its completion write fails — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/environment/translator/openai_chat.go releaseSingleShot (ReleaseSession, then RecordPodRelease, which records nothing on a non-terminal row).
WATCHOUT: the predicate is "any error other than ErrSuperseded", which covers ErrNotFound and context deadlines as well as a store outage. ErrSuperseded means another hook writer ended the row and its hook releases, so endHeldSession must not release then. That existing TEST-1 case ("makes no AfterTerminalWrite call") stays as it is.
WATCHOUT: in TEST-1 the "commit error" row of the Failure-exit table means a failure of the commit Update only. A store whose every Update fails from the commit onward is the new fallback case, and it asserts the opposite (no `failed` write, no AfterTerminalWrite, exactly one executor release).

### [non-spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-3 step 1 applies forwardTarget's Forwarded(ctx) branch ahead of forwardGated only when kind == ForwardKindResponsesTurn; every existing kind keeps the gate-first order. The TEST-1 tier-1 "every other kind" clause is deleted, the summary 0101 Impacts row says "for that kind", and the existing-kind rule-5 gap goes in the summary Defects list — BECAUSE the every-kind reorder makes a forwarded ForwardKindMessageNoReplyTo on a non-running/non-input_required row return ForwardNotDelivered, refuseForward writes nothing for that kind, and both callers DLQ-hold it without checking state, against §29.3 Forwarding rule 6 (hold only for running or input_required) — ALTERNATIVES: keep every-kind reorder and gate the DLQ hold on messageForwardStates in refuseForward and both callers (widens 0108 into 0101's kinds and callers; a separate fix of an existing defect); a lease pre-check in the ForwardResponsesTurn wrapper (second rule-5 copy, rejected round 1); listing every state in forwardStates[ForwardKindResponsesTurn] (breaks the map's meaning); a tier-1 test pinning existing kinds' ForwardLocal on a gated-out forwarded row (pins the defect).
MISTAKE: an earlier round staged the forwardTarget reorder "for every kind" (review-log entries at the round-1 DECISION/WATCHOUT on the reorder) and judged the DLQ-hold side effect "no regression". It is a regression in kind: rule 6 refuses rather than holds on those states, so the reorder converted one rule violation into another and widened 0108 into 0101's kinds.
CORRECTS [review-log WATCHOUT "the reorder changes every kind ... the reorder is no regression"]: the reorder is now scoped to ForwardKindResponsesTurn; existing kinds are unchanged and their rule-5 gap is a Defects-list item, not a staged change.
WATCHOUT: do not re-widen the forwardTarget reorder to every kind without also gating the no-inReplyTo DLQ hold on running/input_required in refuseForward, forwardMessageBatch/holdUndeliveredForward, and MCP lenny/send_message (mcptools_register.go undelivered=true branch). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/holder_forward.go:328-330; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:126-137; pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:324-327; spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:582-593

DECISION: scope the forwardTarget reorder to ForwardKindResponsesTurn. (c) Gate ResponsesTurn on every state: this breaks forwardStates' documented meaning.
CORRECTS [non-spec.1 DECISION "moving forwardTarget's Forwarded(ctx) branch ahead of forwardGated for every kind" and the WATCHOUT "the reorder is no regression"]: the reorder does not regress against today's local serve, but it changes 0101 kinds' outcomes to a rule-6-violating DLQ hold with no test pinning them. "Rule 5 holds for every kind" was false for ForwardKindMessageNoReplyTo. ### [non-spec.4.review-performance.1]
- DECISION: no performance or failure-mode findings in non-spec round 4 — BECAUSE the r3->r4 diff only narrows the forwardTarget reorder to ForwardKindResponsesTurn (one extra leaseStore.Get per forwarded warm turn, none for other kinds) and adds a Defects bullet; a warm turn still costs two session-row Updates plus one transcript append in Postgres, with per-session row and advisory locks only, no etcd write, and no new watch — ALTERNATIVES: filing the lost-commit-ack case (pgstore Update returns an error when writeApplied's read-back times out during failover, so a serveWarm commit can land Completed=true on a row endHeldSession then fails to end) was rejected: the next continuation reserves, gets ErrNotBound from the released binding, and goes cold, so it degrades no worse than the shipped handler.
- FACT: pgstore Update rewrites every column, including the request_envelope jsonb, on every write by any writer, and resolves a lost commit ack by reading last_write_id on a detached context bounded by DefaultPrimaryReadTimeout. If that read fails, it returns the error even when the write committed — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:588-640, 776-805
- FACT: the CH-HOLDERFORWARD transport sets MaxIdleConnsPerHost 16 and no MaxConnsPerHost, and because DialTLSContext is custom it speaks HTTP/1.1. Long streaming warm turns therefore take one connection each and no client-side cap queues them — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/holder_forward.go:666-679
- USEFUL [non-spec.2.review-performance.1], [Standing context: Coordinator adoption, Envelope growth, Required writes]: these covered the Redis-reset, handoff, and envelope-growth traces, and nothing in the r4 diff changes them.

### [non-spec.4.review-reliability.1]
- DECISION: filed nothing — BECAUSE the round-4 diff (forwardTarget reorder scoped to ForwardKindResponsesTurn, tier-1 test trimmed, new Defects entry) is self-consistent and the recovery paths (endHeldSession store-failure fallback, one-way reservation, crash windows, forward unknown outcome) each have a stated outcome and a test — ALTERNATIVES: a stale-coordinator endHeldSession write (§10.1.5) and a missing "other kinds keep current order" test were considered and dropped; the first is consistent with paragraph D plus settleStaleBinding in the hook, the second would pin behavior the summary lists as a defect.
- FACT: the CH-HOLDERFORWARD client has no total or response-header timeout; only dial+TLS are bounded (HolderForwardDialBound), so a forwarded warm turn lives as long as the client request context — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/holder_forward.go:666-697
- FACT: the forward listener's noStoreMarkWriter implements Flush and Unwrap, so a forwarded stream:true warm turn can stream through it — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/holder_forward_listener.go:157-169
- FACT: AfterTerminalWrite runs settleStaleBinding before seal/release, so a terminal write from a replica whose binding went stale evicts without a binder call — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go:94-100
- FACT: hold=false cold path writes the completion after the commit; the client learns S.1 only after both, so the crash window between them is unreachable by a continuation — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-5 step 6 order (record, commit, hold-or-release, respond)
- USEFUL [Standing context: Coordinator adoption, Sealed session refusal, Idempotency]: saved re-deriving the 409 window, the idle-suspension race, and the retry-forks-not-duplicates reasoning.

The calling-session path (CallingSessionHeader, no credential) is reachable only from mTLS-authenticated replicas. FACT: No §4.8 interceptor phase runs on client input at session start or on REST delivery to a running session (PreMessageDelivery is lenny/send_message only), so exec.Send on the warm path bypasses no policy phase. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1449, spec/04_system-components.md:1525

### [non-spec.7.review-performance.1]
FACT: The r5-prefix to current diff is the single tier-1 rule-5 test clause ("no forwarder call" changed to "no `ForwardResponsesTurn` call"). It changes no write path. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md Testing, Tier 1, "Forwarding §29.3 rule 5" bullet
DECISION: No performance finding. BECAUSE a warm turn writes two Postgres session-row Updates (reservation, commit) and one transcript append, makes no etcd write, and does no claim or release, so it costs less than the shipped per-request bind. A cold turn adds one commit Update to the shipped bind, completion, and release, which is O(1) per request. During a Postgres outage, the endHeldSession fallback to releaseSingleShot(DispositionFailed) matches the shipped deferred-release behavior. — ALTERNATIVES: filing O(turns) envelope rewrite amplification was rejected, because Standing context "Envelope growth" settles it.
USEFUL [Standing context: Envelope growth, Coordinator adoption, Transcript storage]: these settled the Redis-reset, handoff, and amplification checks without re-deriving them.

### [non-spec.7.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The CH-HOLDERFORWARD client sets no overall timeout; a forward runs under the caller's request context (rule 8), and the TCP dial and TLS handshake are bounded at HolderForwardDialBound. A long warm turn forwarded to the holder is therefore not cut by the client, and a client disconnect at the serving replica cancels the holder's request context, which the failure-exit context.Canceled test row already covers. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/holder_forward.go:634-680
FACT: forwardToHolder calls refuseForward on every non-Delivered sendForward outcome, Unknown included, before it writes any byte. copyForwardResponse's mid-body Unknown writes no refusal. CODE-3 step 2 and the doc comment match this. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/holder_forward.go:298-337
DECISION: no findings under the reliability lens in round 7. BECAUSE: the only diff since r5 is a one-word test-wording fix ("no ForwardResponsesTurn call"). Reservation CAS, the one-way incomplete mark, endHeldSession (claim, detached bounded context, ErrSuperseded skip, releaseSingleShot fallback), and the tier-8 crash, restore, and forward-fault cases all trace correctly. ALTERNATIVES: considered filing (1) the hold=false cold path, where the separate completed-state write fails after the response commit and leaves a completed S.1 behind a 500 (harmless; a client retry only forks); (2) an Update error in endHeldSession that leaves a running row with its pod released (a store-outage corner, which the owner directive says GC and timers handle). Neither clears the bar.

### [non-spec.7.review-security.1]
FACT: The CH-HOLDERFORWARD listener wraps its inner mux with authmw only (restAuthOpts). The REST listener also runs environmentmw, ratelimitmw, cbmw, and idemmw, and those do not run at the coordinator on a forwarded request. For a forwarded POST /v1/responses that the coordinator serves cold, the §10.6 gate still holds, because sessionserver re-resolves the Resolution on demand when it is absent and fails closed on a store error. Rate limiting and breakers already ran at the serving replica. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/holder_forward_listener.go:76-88; cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go:810-921; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/runtimes.go:379-398,442-458; pkg/gateway/middleware/environment/environment.go:80-88
WATCHOUT: Do not file "the forward listener skips environmentmw, so a coordinator-created cold session bypasses environment admission". The on-demand Resolve fallback refutes it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/runtimes.go:442-458
FACT: Round 7's only diff is the TEST-1 rule-5 wording "no `ForwardResponsesTurn` call". The security lens found nothing in it or in the rest of the non-spec staging. Every bound the warm path uses (recovery_generation, EndSeq, Completed, the hold decision) comes from a gateway-owned row or the gateway's Append, never from a pod. — EVIDENCE: proposals/0108_.../0108_...non-spec-changes.md:247
